<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

### Describe the bug

mode="vertices" in Boxes.from_tensor add +1 to boxes width and height

### Reproduction steps

```python
from kornia.geometry.boxes import Boxes

boxes = torch.tensor([[[355,10, 660,250], [10,125, 200,400]]]) 
vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes).to_tensor('vertices')
assert (vertices == Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")) # <-- fails
```
original vertices
```
tensor([[[[355.,  10.],
          [660.,  10.],
          [660., 250.],
          [355., 250.]],

         [[ 10., 125.],
          [200., 125.],
          [200., 400.],
          [ 10., 400.]]]])


```
after from and to boxes
```
tensor([[[[355.,  10.],
          [661.,  10.],
          [661., 251.],
          [355., 251.]],

         [[ 10., 125.],
          [201., 125.],
          [201., 401.],
          [ 10., 401.]]]])

```


### Expected behavior

To return the same original boxes

### Environment

```shell
kornia 0.6.5
torch 1.9.1+cpu
```


### Additional context

_No response_
</issue_description>

- Ensure backward compatibility
- Don't break existing functionality
- Test edge cases</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue is with the `Boxes.from_tensor` method in the Kornia library. When using `mode="vertices"`:
- The method incorrectly adds +1 to both width and height of bounding boxes
- This causes round-trip conversions (tensor → vertices → tensor) to produce different results
- Specifically, the x2 and y2 coordinates are incremented by 1

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce

1. Create a tensor with box coordinates: `[[355,10, 660,250], [10,125, 200,400]]`
2. Convert to Boxes object: `Boxes.from_tensor(boxes)`
3. Convert back to vertices: `.to_tensor('vertices')`
4. Convert vertices back to boxes with `mode="vertices"`: `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")`
5. Convert back to vertices again: `.to_tensor("vertices")`
6. Compare original vs final result - they should be identical but aren't

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing

- Create minimal reproduction case
- Understand coordinate system conventions (0-based indexing, inclusive/exclusive bounds)
- Ensure fix doesn't break other modes
- Test edge cases (small boxes, zero-area boxes)
- Maintain backward compatibility

    37	*Kornia* is a differentiable computer vision library for [PyTorch](https://pytorch.org).

    39	It consists of a set of routines and differentiable modules to solve generic computer vision problems. At its core, the package uses *PyTorch* as its main backend both for efficiency and to take advantage of the reverse-mode auto-differentiation to define and compute the gradient of complex functions.

    11	def _is_floating_point_dtype(dtype: torch.dtype) -> bool:
    12	    return dtype in (torch.float16, torch.float32, torch.float64, torch.bfloat16, torch.half)

    14	
    15	def _merge_box_list(
    16	    boxes: List[torch.Tensor], method: str = "pad"
    17	) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, List[int]]:
    18	    r"""Merge a list of boxes into one tensor.
    19	    """
    20	    if not all(box.shape[-2:] == torch.Size([4, 2]) and box.dim() == 3 for box in boxes):
    21	        raise TypeError(
    22	            f"Input boxes must be a list of (N, 4, 2) shaped. Got: {[box.shape for box in boxes]}.")
    23	
    24	    if method == "pad":
    25	        max_N = max(box.shape[0] for box in boxes)
    26	        stats = [max_N - box.shape[0] for box in boxes]
    27	        output = torch.nn.utils.rnn.pad_sequence(boxes, batch_first=True)
    28	    else:
    29	        raise NotImplementedError(f"`{method}` is not implemented.")
    30	
    31	    return output, stats

    34	def _transform_boxes(boxes: torch.Tensor, M: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    35	    """Transforms 3D and 2D in kornia format by applying the transformation matrix M. Boxes and the transformation
    36	    matrix could be batched or not.
    37	
    38	    Args:
    39	        boxes: 2D quadrilaterals or 3D hexahedrons in kornia format.
    40	        M: the transformation matrix of shape :math:`(3, 3)` or :math:`(B, 3, 3)` for 2D and :math:`(4, 4)` or
    41	            :math:`(B, 4, 4)` for 3D hexahedron.
    42	    """
    43	    M = M if M.is_floating_point() else M.float()
    44	
    45	    # Work with batch as kornia.transform_points only supports a batch of points.
    46	    boxes_per_batch, n_points_per_box, coordinates_dimension = boxes.shape[-3:]
    47	    points = boxes.view(-1, n_points_per_box * boxes_per_batch, coordinates_dimension)
    48	    M = M if M.ndim == 3 else M.unsqueeze(0)
    49	
    50	    if points.shape[0] != M.shape[0]:
    51	        raise ValueError(
    52	            f"Batch size mismatch. Got {points.shape[0]} for boxes and {M.shape[0]} for the transformation matrix."
    53	        )
    54	
    55	    transformed_boxes: torch.Tensor = transform_points(M, points)
    56	    transformed_boxes = transformed_boxes.view_as(boxes)
    57	    return transformed_boxes
    58	
    59	
    60	def _boxes_to_polygons(
    61	    xmin: torch.Tensor, ymin: torch.Tensor, width: torch.Tensor, height: torch.Tensor
    62	) -> torch.Tensor:
    63	    if not xmin.ndim == ymin.ndim == width.ndim == height.ndim == 2:
    64	        raise ValueError("We expect to create a batch of 2D boxes (quadrilaterals) in vertices format (B, N, 4, 2)")
    65	
    66	    # Create (B,N,4,2) with all points in top left position of boxes
    67	    polygons = torch.zeros((xmin.shape[0], xmin.shape[1], 4, 2), device=xmin.device, dtype=xmin.dtype)
    68	    polygons[..., 0] = xmin.unsqueeze(-1)
    69	    polygons[..., 1] = ymin.unsqueeze(-1)
    70	    # Shift top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left points to the right coordinates
    71	    polygons[..., 1, 0] += width - 1  # Top right
    72	    polygons[..., 2, 0] += width - 1  # Bottom right
    73	    polygons[..., 2, 1] += height - 1  # Bottom right
    74	    polygons[..., 3, 1] += height - 1  # Bottom left
    75	    return polygons
    76	
    77	
    78	def _boxes_to_quadrilaterals(
    79	    boxes: torch.Tensor, mode: str = "xyxy", validate_boxes: bool = True
    80	) -> torch.Tensor:
    81	    """Convert from boxes to quadrilaterals."""
    82	    mode = mode.lower()
    83	
    84	    if mode.startswith("vertices"):
    85	        batched = boxes.ndim == 4
    86	        if not (3 <= boxes.ndim <= 4 and boxes.shape[-2:] == torch.Size([4, 2])):
    87	            raise ValueError(f"Boxes shape must be (N, 4, 2) or (B, N, 4, 2) when {mode} mode. Got {boxes.shape}.")
    88	    elif mode.startswith("xy"):
    89	        batched = boxes.ndim == 3
    90	        if not (2 <= boxes.ndim <= 3 and boxes.shape[-1] == 4):
    91	            raise ValueError(f"Boxes shape must be (N, 4) or (B, N, 4) when {mode} mode. Got {boxes.shape}.")
    92	    else:
    93	        raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
    94	
    95	    boxes = boxes if boxes.is_floating_point() else boxes.float()
    96	    boxes = boxes if batched else boxes.unsqueeze(0)
    97	
    98	    if mode.startswith("vertices"):
    99	        if mode == "vertices":
   100	            # Avoid passing reference
   101	            quadrilaterals = boxes.clone()
   102	        elif mode == "vertices_plus":
   103	            quadrilaterals = boxes.clone()  # TODO: perform +1
   104	        else:
   105	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   106	        validate_boxes or validate_bbox(quadrilaterals)
   107	    elif mode.startswith("xy"):
   108	        if mode == "xyxy":
   109	            height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1], boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0]
   110	        elif mode == "xyxy_plus":
   111	            height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1, boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
   112	        elif mode == "xywh":
   113	            height, width = boxes[..., 3], boxes[..., 2]
   114	        else:
   115	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   116	
   117	        if validate_boxes:
   118	            if (width <= 0).any():
   119	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative widths or 0.")
   120	            if (height <= 0).any():
   121	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative heights or 0.")
   122	
   123	        xmin, ymin = boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1]
   124	        quadrilaterals = _boxes_to_polygons(xmin, ymin, width, height)
   125	    else:
   126	        raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   127	
   128	    quadrilaterals = quadrilaterals if batched else quadrilaterals.squeeze(0)
   129	
   130	    return quadrilaterals
   131	
   132	
   133	def _boxes3d_to_polygons3d(
   134	    xmin: torch.Tensor,
   135	    ymin: torch.Tensor,
   136	    zmin: torch.Tensor,
   137	    width: torch.Tensor,
   138	    height: torch.Tensor,
   139	    depth: torch.Tensor,
   140	) -> torch.Tensor:
   141	    if not xmin.ndim == ymin.ndim == zmin.ndim == width.ndim == height.ndim == depth.ndim == 2:
   142	        raise ValueError("We expect to create a batch of 3D boxes (hexahedrons) in vertices format (B, N, 8, 3)")
   143	
   144	    # Front
   145	    # Create (B,N,4,3) with all points in front top left position of boxes
   146	    front_vertices = torch.zeros((xmin.shape[0], xmin.shape[1], 4, 3), device=xmin.device, dtype=xmin.dtype)
   147	    front_vertices[..., 0] = xmin.unsqueeze(-1)
   148	    front_vertices[..., 1] = ymin.unsqueeze(-1)
   149	    front_vertices[..., 2] = zmin.unsqueeze(-1)
   150	    # Shift front-top-right, front-bottom-right, front-bottom-left points to the right coordinates
   151	    front_vertices[..., 1, 0] += width - 1  # Top right
   152	    front_vertices[..., 2, 0] += width - 1  # Bottom right
   153	    front_vertices[..., 2, 1] += height - 1  # Bottom right
   154	    front_vertices[..., 3, 1] += height - 1  # Bottom left
   155	
   156	    # Back
   157	    back_vertices = front_vertices.clone()
   158	    back_vertices[..., 2] += depth.unsqueeze(-1) - 1
   159	
   160	    polygons3d = torch.cat([front_vertices, back_vertices], dim=-2)
   161	    return polygons3d
   162

   164	# NOTE: Cannot jit with Union types with torch <= 0.10
   165	# @torch.jit.script
   166	class Boxes:
   167	    r"""2D boxes containing N or BxN boxes.
   168	
   169	    Args:
   170	        boxes: 2D boxes, shape of :math:`(N, 4, 2)`, :math:`(B, N, 4, 2)` or a list of :math:`(N, 4, 2)`.
   171	            See below for more details.
   172	        raise_if_not_floating_point: flag to control floating point casting behaviour when `boxes` is not a floating
   173	            point tensor. True to raise an error when `boxes` isn't a floating point tensor, False to cast to float.
   174	        mode: the box format of the input boxes.
   175	
   176	    Note:
   177	        **2D boxes format** is defined as a floating data type tensor of shape ``Nx4x2`` or ``BxNx4x2``
   178	        where each box is a `quadrilateral <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrilateral>`_ defined by it's 4 vertices
   179	        coordinates (A, B, C, D). Coordinates must be in ``x, y`` order. The height and width of a box is defined as
   180	        ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``. Examples of
   181	        `quadrilaterals <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrilateral>`_ are rectangles, rhombus and trapezoids.
   182	    """
   183	    def __init__(
   184	        self, boxes: Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]], raise_if_not_floating_point: bool = True,
   185	        mode: str = "vertices_plus"
   186	    ) -> None:
   187	
   188	        self._N: Optional[List[int]] = None
   189	
   190	        if isinstance(boxes, list):
   191	            boxes, self._N = _merge_box_list(boxes)
   192	
   193	        if not isinstance(boxes, torch.Tensor):
   194	            raise TypeError(f"Input boxes is not a Tensor. Got: {type(boxes)}.")
   195	
   196	        if not boxes.is_floating_point():
   197	            if raise_if_not_floating_point:
   198	                raise ValueError(f"Coordinates must be in floating point. Got {boxes.dtype}")
   199	
   200	            boxes = boxes.float()
   201	
   202	        if len(boxes.shape) == 0:
   203	            # Use reshape, so we don't end up creating a new tensor that does not depend on
   204	            # the inputs (and consequently confuses jit)
   205	            boxes = boxes.reshape((-1, 4))
   206	
   207	        if not (3 <= boxes.ndim <= 4 and boxes.shape[-2:] == (4, 2)):
   208	            raise ValueError(f"Boxes shape must be (N, 4, 2) or (B, N, 4, 2). Got {boxes.shape}.")
   209	
   210	        self._is_batched = False if boxes.ndim == 3 else True
   211	
   212	        self._data = boxes
   213	        self._mode = mode
   214	
   215	    def get_boxes_shape(self) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
   216	        r"""Compute boxes heights and widths.
   217	
   218	        Returns:
   219	            - Boxes heights, shape of :math:`(N,)` or :math:`(B,N)`.
   220	            - Boxes widths, shape of :math:`(N,)` or :math:`(B,N)`.
   221	
   222	        Example:
   223	            >>> boxes_xyxy = torch.tensor([[[1,1,2,2],[1,1,3,2]]])
   224	            >>> boxes = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes_xyxy)
   225	            >>> boxes.get_boxes_shape()
   226	            (tensor([[1., 1.]]), tensor([[1., 2.]]))
   227	        """
   228	        boxes_xywh = cast(torch.Tensor, self.to_tensor("xywh", as_padded_sequence=True))
   229	        widths, heights = boxes_xywh[..., 2], boxes_xywh[..., 3]
   230	        return heights, widths
   231	
   232	    @classmethod
   233	    def from_tensor(
   234	        cls, boxes: Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]], mode: str = "xyxy", validate_boxes: bool = True
   235	    ) -> "Boxes":
   236	        r"""Helper method to easily create :class:`Boxes` from boxes stored in another format.
   237	
   238	        Args:
   239	            boxes: 2D boxes, shape of :math:`(N, 4)`, :math:`(B, N, 4)`, :math:`(N, 4, 2)` or :math:`(B, N, 4, 2)`.
   240	            mode: The format in which the boxes are provided.
   241	
   242	                * 'xyxy': boxes are assumed to be in the format ``xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax`` where ``width = xmax - xmin``
   243	                  and ``height = ymax - ymin``. With shape :math:`(N, 4)`, :math:`(B, N, 4)`.
   244	                * 'xyxy_plus': similar to 'xyxy' mode but where box width and length are defined as
   245	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``.
   246	                  With shape :math:`(N, 4)`, :math:`(B, N, 4)`.
   247	                * 'xywh': boxes are assumed to be in the format ``xmin, ymin, width, height`` where
   248	                  ``width = xmax - xmin`` and ``height = ymax - ymin``. With shape :math:`(N, 4)`, :math:`(B, N, 4)`.
   249	                * 'vertices': boxes are defined by their vertices points in the following ``clockwise`` order:
   250	                  *top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left*. Vertices coordinates are in (x,y) order. Finally,
   251	                  box width and height are defined as ``width = xmax - xmin`` and ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   252	                  With shape :math:`(N, 4, 2)` or :math:`(B, N, 4, 2)`.
   253	                * 'vertices_plus': similar to 'vertices' mode but where box width and length are defined as
   254	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``. ymin + 1``.
   255	                  With shape :math:`(N, 4, 2)` or :math:`(B, N, 4, 2)`.
   256	
   257	            validate_boxes: check if boxes are valid rectangles or not. Valid rectangles are those with width
   258	                and height >= 1 (>= 2 when mode ends with '_plus' suffix).
   259	
   260	        Returns:
   261	            :class:`Boxes` class containing the original `boxes` in the format specified by ``mode``.
   262	
   263	        Examples:
   264	            >>> boxes_xyxy = torch.as_tensor([[0, 3, 1, 4], [5, 1, 8, 4]])
   265	            >>> boxes = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes_xyxy, mode='xyxy')
   266	            >>> boxes.data  # (2, 4, 2)
   267	            tensor([[[0., 3.],
   268	                     [0., 3.],
   269	                     [0., 3.],
   270	                     [0., 3.]],
   271	            <BLANKLINE>
   272	                    [[5., 1.],
   273	                     [7., 1.],
   274	                     [7., 3.],
   275	                     [5., 3.]]])
   276	        """
   277	        quadrilaterals: Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]]
   278	        if isinstance(boxes, (torch.Tensor)):
   279	            quadrilaterals = _boxes_to_quadrilaterals(boxes, mode=mode, validate_boxes=validate_boxes)
   280	        else:
   281	            quadrilaterals = [_boxes_to_quadrilaterals(box, mode, validate_boxes) for box in boxes]
   282	
   283	        # Due to some torch.jit.script bug (at least <= 1.9), you need to pass all arguments to __init__ when
   284	        # constructing the class from inside of a method.
   285	        return cls(quadrilaterals, False, mode)
   286	
   287	    def to_tensor(
   288	        self, mode: Optional[str] = None, as_padded_sequence: bool = False
   289	    ) -> Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]]:
   290	        r"""Cast :class:`Boxes` to a tensor. ``mode`` controls which 2D boxes format should be use to represent boxes
   291	        in the tensor.
   292	
   293	        Args:
   294	            mode: the output box format. It could be:
   295	
   296	                * 'xyxy': boxes are defined as ``xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax`` where ``width = xmax - xmin`` and
   297	                  ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   298	                * 'xyxy_plus': similar to 'xyxy' mode but where box width and length are defined as
   299	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``.
   300	                * 'xywh': boxes are defined as ``xmin, ymin, width, height`` where ``width = xmax - xmin``
   301	                  and ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   302	                * 'vertices': boxes are defined by their vertices points in the following ``clockwise`` order:
   303	                  *top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left*. Vertices coordinates are in (x,y) order. Finally,
   304	                  box width and height are defined as ``width = xmax - xmin`` and ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   305	                * 'vertices_plus': similar to 'vertices' mode but where box width and length are defined as
   306	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``. ymin + 1``.
   307	            as_padded_sequence: whether to keep the pads for a list of boxes. This parameter is only valid
   308	                if the boxes are from a box list.
   309	
   310	        Returns:
   311	            Boxes tensor in the ``mode`` format. The shape depends with the ``mode`` value:
   312	
   313	                * 'vertices' or 'verticies_plus': :math:`(N, 4, 2)` or :math:`(B, N, 4, 2)`.
   314	                * Any other value: :math:`(N, 4)` or :math:`(B, N, 4)`.
   315	
   316	        Examples:
   317	            >>> boxes_xyxy = torch.as_tensor([[0, 3, 1, 4], [5, 1, 8, 4]])
   318	            >>> boxes = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes_xyxy)
   319	            >>> assert (boxes_xyxy == boxes.to_tensor(mode='xyxy')).all()
   320	        """

   322	
   323	        boxes: Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]]
   324	
   325	        # Create boxes in xyxy_plus format.
   326	        boxes = torch.stack([batched_boxes.amin(dim=-2), batched_boxes.amax(dim=-2)], dim=-2).view(
   327	            batched_boxes.shape[0], batched_boxes.shape[1], 4
   328	        )
   329	
   330	        if mode is None:
   331	            mode = self.mode
   332	
   333	        mode = mode.lower()
   334	
   335	        if mode in ("xyxy", "xyxy_plus"):
   336	            pass
   337	        elif mode in ("xywh", "vertices", "vertices_plus"):
   338	            height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1, boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
   339	            boxes[..., 2] = width
   340	            boxes[..., 3] = height
   341	        else:
   342	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   343	
   344	        if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):
   345	            offset = torch.as_tensor([0, 0, 1, 1], device=boxes.device, dtype=boxes.dtype)
   346	            boxes = boxes + offset
   347	
   348	        if mode.startswith('vertices'):
   349	            boxes = _boxes_to_polygons(boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1], boxes[..., 2], boxes[..., 3])
   350	
   351	        if self._N is not None and not as_padded_sequence:
   352	            boxes = list(torch.nn.functional.p<response clipped><NOTE>Due to the max output limit, only part of this file has been shown to you.    358	    def to_mask(self, height: int, width: int) -> torch.Tensor:
   359	        """Convert 2D boxes to masks. Covered area is 1 and the remaining is 0.
   360	
   361	        Args:
   362	            height: height of the masked image/images.
   363	            width: width of the masked image/images.
   364	
   365	        Returns:
   366	            the output mask tensor, shape of :math:`(N, width, height)` or :math:`(B,N, width, height)` and dtype of
   367	            :func:`Boxes.dtype` (it can be any floating point dtype).
   368	
   369	        Note:
   370	            It is currently non-differentiable.
   371	
   372	        Examples:
   373	            >>> boxes = Boxes(torch.tensor([[  # Equivalent to boxes = Boxes.from_tensor([[1,1,4,3]])
   374	            ...        [1., 1.],
   375	            ...        [4., 1.],
   376	            ...        [4., 3.],
   377	            ...        [1., 3.],
   378	            ...   ]]))  # 1x4x2
   379	            >>> boxes.to_mask(5, 5)
   380	            tensor([[[0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   381	                     [0., 1., 1., 1., 1.],
   382	                     [0., 1., 1., 1., 1.],
   383	                     [0., 1., 1., 1., 1.],
   384	                     [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.]]])
   385	        """
   386	        if self._data.requires_grad:
   387	            raise RuntimeError(
   388	                "Boxes.to_tensor isn't differentiable. Please, create boxes from tensors with `requires_grad=False`."
   389	            )
   390	
   391	        if self._is_batched:  # (B, N, 4, 2)
   392	            mask = torch.zeros(
   393	                (self._data.shape[0], self._data.shape[1], height, width), dtype=self.dtype, device=self.device
   394	            )
   395	        else:  # (N, 4, 2)
   396	            mask = torch.zeros((self._data.shape[0], height, width), dtype=self.dtype, device=self.device)
   397	
   398	        # Boxes coordinates can be outside the image size after transforms. Clamp values to the image size
   399	        clipped_boxes_xyxy = cast(torch.Tensor, self.to_tensor("xyxy", as_padded_sequence=True))
   400	        clipped_boxes_xyxy[..., ::2].clamp_(0, width)
   401	        clipped_boxes_xyxy[..., 1::2].clamp_(0, height)
   402	
   403	        # Reshape mask to (BxN, H, W) and boxes to (BxN, 4) to iterate over all of them.
   404	        # Cast boxes coordinates to be integer to use them as indexes. Use round to handle decimal values.
   405	        for mask_channel, box_xyxy in zip(mask.view(-1, height, width), clipped_boxes_xyxy.view(-1, 4).round().int()):
   406	            # Mask channel dimensions: (height, width)
   407	            mask_channel[box_xyxy[1] : box_xyxy[3], box_xyxy[0] : box_xyxy[2]] = 1
   408	
   409	        return mask
   410	
   411	    def transform_boxes(self, M: torch.Tensor, inplace: bool = False) -> "Boxes":
   412	        r"""Apply a transformation matrix to the 2D boxes.
   413	
   414	        Args:
   415	            M: The transformation matrix to be applied, shape of :math:`(3, 3)` or :math:`(B, 3, 3)`.
   416	            inplace: do transform in-place and return self.
   417	
   418	        Returns:
   419	            The transformed boxes.
   420	        """
   421	        if not 2 <= M.ndim <= 3 or M.shape[-2:] != (3, 3):
   422	            raise ValueError(f"The transformation matrix shape must be (3, 3) or (B, 3, 3). Got {M.shape}.")
   423	
   424	        # Due to some torch.jit.script bug (at least <= 1.9), you need to pass all arguments to __init__ when
   425	        # constructing the class from inside of a method.
   426	        transformed_boxes = _transform_boxes(self._data, M)
   427	        if inplace:
   428	            self._data = transformed_boxes
   429	            return self
   430	
   431	        return Boxes(transformed_boxes, False)
   432	
   433	    def transform_boxes_(self, M: torch.Tensor) -> "Boxes":
   434	        """Inplace version of :func:`Boxes.transform_boxes`"""
   435	        return self.transform_boxes(M, inplace=True)
   436	
   437	    @property
   438	    def data(self) -> torch.Tensor:
   439	        return self._data
   440	
   441	    @property
   442	    def mode(self) -> str:
   443	        return self._mode
   444	
   445	    @property
   446	    def device(self) -> torch.device:
   447	        """Returns boxes device."""
   448	        return self._data.device

   451	    def dtype(self) -> torch.dtype:

   455	    def to(self, device: Optional[torch.device] = None, dtype: Optional[torch.dtype] = None) -> "Boxes":
   456	        """Like :func:`torch.nn.Module.to()` method."""
   457	        # In torchscript, dtype is a int and not a class. https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/51941
   458	        if dtype is not None and not _is_floating_point_dtype(dtype):
   459	            raise ValueError("Boxes must be in floating point")
   460	        self._data = self._data.to(device=device, dtype=dtype)
   461	        return self

   464	@torch.jit.script
   465	class Boxes3D:
   466	    r"""3D boxes containing N or BxN boxes.
   467	
   468	    Args:
   469	        boxes: 3D boxes, shape of :math:`(N,8,3)` or :math:`(B,N,8,3)`. See below for more details.
   470	        raise_if_not_floating_point: flag to control floating point casting behaviour when `boxes` is not a floating
   471	            point tensor. True to raise an error when `boxes` isn't a floating point tensor, False to cast to float.
   472	
   473	    Note:
   474	        **3D boxes format** is defined as a floating data type tensor of shape ``Nx8x3`` or ``BxNx8x3`` where each box
   475	        is a `hexahedron <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hexahedron>`_ defined by it's 8 vertices coordinates.
   476	        Coordinates must be in ``x, y, z`` order. The height, width and depth of a box is defined as
   477	        ``width = xmax - xmin + 1``, ``height = ymax - ymin + 1`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin + 1``. Examples of
   478	        `hexahedrons <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hexahedron>`_ are cubes and rhombohedrons.
   479	    """
   480	    def __init__(
   481	        self, boxes: torch.Tensor, raise_if_not_floating_point: bool = True,
   482	        mode: str = "xyzxyz_plus"
   483	    ) -> None:
   484	        if not isinstance(boxes, torch.Tensor):
   485	            raise TypeError(f"Input boxes is not a Tensor. Got: {type(boxes)}.")
   486	
   487	        if not boxes.is_floating_point():
   488	            if raise_if_not_floating_point:
   489	                raise ValueError(f"Coordinates must be in floating point. Got {boxes.dtype}.")
   490	
   491	            boxes = boxes.float()
   492	
   493	        if len(boxes.shape) == 0:
   494	            # Use reshape, so we don't end up creating a new tensor that does not depend on
   495	            # the inputs (and consequently confuses jit)
   496	            boxes = boxes.reshape((-1, 6))
   497	
   498	        if not (3 <= boxes.ndim <= 4 and boxes.shape[-2:] == (8, 3)):
   499	            raise ValueError(f"3D bbox shape must be (N, 8, 3) or (B, N, 8, 3). Got {boxes.shape}.")
   500	
   501	        self._is_batched = False if boxes.ndim == 3 else True
   502	
   503	        self._data = boxes
   504	        self._mode = mode
   505	
   506	    def get_boxes_shape(self) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
   507	        r"""Compute boxes heights and widths.
   508	
   509	        Returns:
   510	            - Boxes depths, shape of :math:`(N,)` or :math:`(B,N)`.
   511	            - Boxes heights, shape of :math:`(N,)` or :math:`(B,N)`.
   512	            - Boxes widths, shape of :math:`(N,)` or :math:`(B,N)`.
   513	
   514	        Example:
   515	            >>> boxes_xyzxyz = torch.tensor([[ 0,  1,  2, 10, 21, 32], [3, 4, 5, 43, 54, 65]])
   516	            >>> boxes3d = Boxes3D.from_tensor(boxes_xyzxyz)
   517	            >>> boxes3d.get_boxes_shape()
   518	            (tensor([30., 60.]), tensor([20., 50.]), tensor([10., 40.]))
   519	        """
   520	        boxes_xyzwhd = self.to_tensor(mode='xyzwhd')
   521	        widths, heights, depths = boxes_xyzwhd[..., 3], boxes_xyzwhd[..., 4], boxes_xyzwhd[..., 5]
   522	        return depths, heights, widths
   523	
   524	    @classmethod
   525	    def from_tensor(cls, boxes: torch.Tensor, mode: str = "xyzxyz", validate_boxes: bool = True) -> "Boxes3D":
   526	        r"""Helper method to easily create :class:`Boxes3D` from 3D boxes stored in another format.
   527	
   528	        Args:
   529	            boxes: 3D boxes, shape of :math:`(N,6)` or :math:`(B,N,6)`.
   530	            mode: The format in which the 3D boxes are provided.
   531	
   532	                * 'xyzxyz': boxes are assumed to be in the format ``xmin, ymin, zmin, xmax, ymax, zmax`` where
   533	                  ``width = xmax - xmin``, ``height = ymax - ymin`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin``.
   534	                * 'xyzxyz_plus': similar to 'xyzxyz' mode but where box width, length and depth are defined as
   535	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1``, ``height = ymax - ymin + 1`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin + 1``.
   536	                * 'xyzwhd': boxes are assumed to be in the format ``xmin, ymin, zmin, width, height, depth`` where
   537	                  ``width = xmax - xmin``, ``height = ymax - ymin`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin``.
   538	
   539	            validate_boxes: check if boxes are valid rectangles or not. Valid rectangles are those with width, height
   540	                and depth >= 1 (>= 2 when mode ends with '_plus' suffix).
   541	
   542	        Returns:
   543	            :class:`Boxes3D` class containing the original `boxes` in the format specified by ``mode``.
   544	
   545	        Examples:
   546	            >>> boxes_xyzxyz = torch.as_tensor([[0, 3, 6, 1, 4, 8], [5, 1, 3, 8, 4, 9]])
   547	            >>> boxes = Boxes3D.from_tensor(boxes_xyzxyz, mode='xyzxyz')
   548	            >>> boxes.data  # (2, 8, 3)
   549	            tensor([[[0., 3., 6.],
   550	                     [0., 3., 6.],
   551	                     [0., 3., 6.],
   552	                     [0., 3., 6.],
   553	                     [0., 3., 7.],
   554	                     [0., 3., 7.],
   555	                     [0., 3., 7.],
   556	                     [0., 3., 7.]],
   557	            <BLANKLINE>
   558	                    [[5., 1., 3.],
   559	                     [7., 1., 3.],
   560	                     [7., 3., 3.],
   561	                     [5., 3., 3.],
   562	                     [5., 1., 8.],
   563	                     [7., 1., 8.],
   564	                     [7., 3., 8.],
   565	                     [5., 3., 8.]]])
   566	        """
   567	        if not (2 <= boxes.ndim <= 3 and boxes.shape[-1] == 6):
   568	            raise ValueError(f"BBox shape must be (N, 6) or (B, N, 6). Got {boxes.shape}.")
   569	
   570	        batched = boxes.ndim == 3
   571	        boxes = boxes if batched else boxes.unsqueeze(0)
   572	        boxes = boxes if boxes.is_floating_point() else boxes.float()
   573	
   574	        xmin, ymin, zmin = boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1], boxes[..., 2]
   575	        mode = mode.lower()
   576	        if mode == "xyzxyz":
   577	            width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 0]
   578	            height = boxes[..., 4] - boxes[..., 1]
   579	            depth = boxes[..., 5] - boxes[..., 2]
   580	        elif mode == "xyzxyz_plus":
   581	            width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
   582	            height = boxes[..., 4] - boxes[..., 1] + 1
   583	            depth = boxes[..., 5] - boxes[..., 2] + 1
   584	        elif mode == "xyzwhd":
   585	            depth, height, width = boxes[..., 4], boxes[..., 3], boxes[..., 5]
   586	        else:
   587	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   588	
   589	        if validate_boxes:
   590	            if (width <= 0).any():
   591	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative widths or 0.")
   592	            if (height <= 0).any():
   593	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative heights or 0.")
   594	            if (depth <= 0).any():
   595	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative depths or 0.")
   596	
   597	        hexahedrons = _boxes3d_to_polygons3d(xmin, ymin, zmin, width, height, depth)
   598	        hexahedrons = hexahedrons if batched else hexahedrons.squeeze(0)
   599	        # Due to some torch.jit.script bug (at least <= 1.9), you need to pass all arguments to __init__ when
   600	        # constructing the class from inside of a method.
   601	        return cls(hexahedrons, raise_if_not_floating_point=False, mode=mode)
   602	
   603	    def to_tensor(self, mode: str = "xyzxyz") -> torch.Tensor:
   604	        r"""Cast :class:`Boxes3D` to a tensor. ``mode`` controls which 3D boxes format should be use to represent boxes
   605	        in the tensor.
   606	
   607	        Args:
   608	            mode: The format in which the boxes are provided.
   609	
   610	                * 'xyzxyz': boxes are assumed to be in the format ``xmin, ymin, zmin, xmax, ymax, zmax`` where
   611	                  ``width = xmax - xmin``, ``height = ymax - ymin`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin``.
   612	                * 'xyzxyz_plus': similar to 'xyzxyz' mode but where box width, length and depth are defined as
   613	                   ``width = xmax - xmin + 1``, ``height = ymax - ymin + 1`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin + 1``.
   614	                * 'xyzwhd': boxes are assumed to be in the format ``xmin, ymin, zmin, width, height, depth`` where
   615	                  ``width = xmax - xmin``, ``height = ymax - ymin`` and ``depth = zmax - zmin``.
   616	                * 'vertices': boxes are defined by their vertices points in the following ``clockwise`` order:
   617	                  *front-top-left, front-top-right, front-bottom-right, front-bottom-left, back-top-left,
   618	                  back-top-right, back-bottom-right,  back-bottom-left*. Vertices coordinates are in (x,y, z) order.
   619	                  Finally, box width, height and depth are defined as ``width = xmax - xmin``, ``height = ymax - ymin``
   620	                  and ``depth = zmax - zmin``.
   621	                * 'vertices_plus': similar to 'vertices' mode but where box width, length and depth are defined as
   622	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``.
   623	
   624	        Returns:
   625	            3D Boxes tensor in the ``mode`` format. The shape depends with the ``mode`` value:
   626	
   627	                * 'vertices' or 'verticies_plus': :math:`(N, 8, 3)` or :math:`(B, N, 8, 3)`.
   628	                * Any other value: :math:`(N, 6)` or :math:`(B, N, 6)`.
   629	
   630	        Note:
   631	            It is currently non-differentiable due to a bug. See github issue
   632	            `#1304 <https://github.com/kornia/kornia/issues/1396>`_.
   633	
   634	        Examples:
   635	            >>> boxes_xyzxyz = torch.as_tensor([[0, 3, 6, 1, 4, 8], [5, 1, 3, 8, 4, 9]])
   636	            >>> boxes = Boxes3D.from_tensor(boxes_xyzxyz, mode='xyzxyz')
   637	            >>> assert (boxes.to_tensor(mode='xyzxyz') == boxes_xyzxyz).all()
   638	        """
   639	        if self._data.requires_grad:
   640	            raise RuntimeError("Boxes3D.to_tensor doesn't support computing gradients since they aren't accurate. "
   641	                               "Please, create boxes from tensors with `requires_grad=False`. "
   642	                               "This is a known bug. Help is needed to fix it. For more information, "
   643	                               "see https://github.com/kornia/kornia/issues/1396.")
   644	
   645	        batched_boxes = self._data if self._is_batched else self._data.unsqueeze(0)
   646	
   647	        # Create boxes in xyzxyz_plus format.
   648	        boxes = torch.stack([batched_boxes.amin(dim=-2), batched_boxes.amax(dim=-2)], dim=-2).view(
   649	            batched_boxes.shape[0], batched_boxes.shape[1], 6
   650	        )
   651	
   652	        mode = mode.lower()
   653	        if mode in ("xyzxyz", "xyzxyz_plus"):
   654	            pass
   655	        elif mode in ("xyzwhd", "vertices", "vertices_plus"):
   656	            width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
   657	            height = boxes[..., 4] - boxes[..., 1] + 1
   658	            depth = boxes[..., 5] - boxes[..., 2] + 1
   659	            boxes[..., 3] = width
   660	            boxes[..., 4] = height
   661	            boxes[..., 5] = depth
   662	        else:
   663	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   664	
   665	        if mode in ("xyzxyz", "vertices"):
   666	            offset = torch.as_tensor([0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1], device=boxes.device, dtype=boxes.dtype)
   667	            boxes = boxes + offset
   668	
   669	        if mode.startswith('vertices'):
   670	            xmin, ymin, zmin = boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1], boxes[..., 2]
   671	            width, height, depth = boxes[..., 3], boxes[..., 4], boxes[..., 5]
   672	
   673	            boxes = _boxes3d_to_polygons3d(xmin, ymin, zmin, width, height, depth)
   674	
   675	        boxes = boxes if self._is_batched else boxes.squeeze(0)
   676	        return boxes
   677	
   678	    def to_mask(self, depth: int, height: int, width: int) -> torch.Tensor:
   679	        """Convert ·D boxes to masks. Covered area is 1 and the remaining is 0.
   680	
   681	        Args:
   682	            depth: depth of the masked image/images.
   683	            height: height of the masked image/images.
   684	            width: width of the masked image/images.
   685	
   686	        Returns:
   687	            the output mask tensor, shape of :math:`(N, depth, width, height)` or :math:`(B,N, depth, width, height)`
   688	             and dtype of :func:`Boxes3D.dtype` (it can be any floating point dtype).
   689	
   690	        Note:
   691	            It is currently non-differentiable.
   692	
   693	        Examples:
   694	            >>> boxes = Boxes3D(torch.tensor([[  # Equivalent to boxes = Boxes.3Dfrom_tensor([[1,1,1,3,3,2]])
   695	            ...     [1., 1., 1.],
   696	            ...     [3., 1., 1.],
   697	            ...     [3., 3., 1.],
   698	            ...     [1., 3., 1.],
   699	            ...     [1., 1., 2.],
   700	            ...     [3., 1., 2.],
   701	            ...     [3., 3., 2.],
   702	            ...     [1., 3., 2.],
   703	            ... ]]))  # 1x8x3
   704	            >>> boxes.to_mask(4, 5, 5)
   705	            tensor([[[[0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   706	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   707	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   708	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   709	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.]],
   710	            <BLANKLINE>
   711	                     [[0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   712	                      [0., 1., 1., 1., 0.],
   713	                      [0., 1., 1., 1., 0.],
   714	                      [0., 1., 1., 1., 0.],
   715	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.]],
   716	            <BLANKLINE>
   717	                     [[0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   718	                      [0., 1., 1., 1., 0.],
   719	                      [0., 1., 1., 1., 0.],
   720	                      [0., 1., 1., 1., 0.],
   721	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.]],
   722	            <BLANKLINE>
   723	                     [[0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   724	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   725	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   726	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.],
   727	                      [0., 0., 0., 0., 0.]]]])
   728	        """
   729	        if self._data.requires_grad:
   730	            raise RuntimeError(
   731	                "Boxes.to_tensor isn't differentiable. Please, create boxes from tensors with `requires_grad=False`."
   732	            )
   733	
   734	        if self._is_batched:  # (B, N, 8, 3)
   735	            mask = torch.zeros(
   736	                (self._data.shape[0], self._data.shape[1], depth, height, width),
   737	                dtype=self._data.dtype,
   738	                device=self._data.device,
   739	            )
   740	        else:  # (N, 8, 3)
   741	            mask = torch.zeros(
   742	                (self._data.shape[0], depth, height, width), dtype=self._data.dtype, device=self._data.device
   743	            )
   744	
   745	        # Boxes coordinates can be outside the image size after transforms. Clamp values to the image size
   746	        clipped_boxes_xyzxyz = self.to_tensor("xyzxyz")
   747	        clipped_boxes_xyzxyz[..., ::3].clamp_(0, width)
   748	        clipped_boxes_xyzxyz[..., 1::3].clamp_(0, height)
   749	        clipped_boxes_xyzxyz[..., 2::3].clamp_(0, depth)
   750	
   751	        # Reshape mask to (BxN, D, H, W) and boxes to (BxN, 6) to iterate over all of them.
   752	        # Cast boxes coordinates to be integer to use them as indexes. Use round to handle decimal values.
   753	        for mask_channel, box_xyzxyz in zip(
   754	            mask.view(-1, depth, height, width), clipped_boxes_xyzxyz.view(-1, 6).round().int()
   755	        ):
   756	            # Mask channel dimensions: (depth, height, width)
   757	            mask_channel[
   758	                box_xyzxyz[2] : box_xyzxyz[5], box_xyzxyz[1] : box_xyzxyz[4], box_xyzxyz[0] : box_xyzxyz[3]
   759	            ] = 1
   760	
   761	        return mask
   762	
   763	    def transform_boxes(self, M: torch.Tensor, inplace: bool = False) -> "Boxes3D":
   764	        r"""Apply a transformation matrix to the 3D boxes.
   765	
   766	        Args:
   767	            M: The transformation matrix to be applied, shape of :math:`(4, 4)` or :math:`(B, 4, 4)`.
   768	            inplace: do transform in-place and return self.
   769	
   770	        Returns:
   771	            The transformed boxes.
   772	        """
   773	        if not 2 <= M.ndim <= 3 or M.shape[-2:] != (4, 4):
   774	            raise ValueError(f"The transformation matrix shape must be (4, 4) or (B, 4, 4). Got {M.shape}.")
   775	
   776	        # Due to some torch.jit.script bug (at least <= 1.9), you need to pass all arguments to __init__ when
   777	        # constructing the class from inside of a method.
   778	        transformed_boxes = _transform_boxes(self._data, M)
   779	        if inplace:
   780	            self._data = transformed_boxes
   781	            return self
   782	
   783	        return Boxes3D(transformed_boxes, False, "xyzxyz_plus")

    78	def _boxes_to_quadrilaterals(
    79	    boxes: torch.Tensor, mode: str = "xyxy", validate_boxes: bool = True
    80	) -> torch.Tensor:
    81	    """Convert from boxes to quadrilaterals."""
    82	    mode = mode.lower()
    83	
    84	    if mode.startswith("vertices"):
    85	        batched = boxes.ndim == 4
    86	        if not (3 <= boxes.ndim <= 4 and boxes.shape[-2:] == torch.Size([4, 2])):
    87	            raise ValueError(f"Boxes shape must be (N, 4, 2) or (B, N, 4, 2) when {mode} mode. Got {boxes.shape}.")
    88	    elif mode.startswith("xy"):
    89	        batched = boxes.ndim == 3
    90	        if not (2 <= boxes.ndim <= 3 and boxes.shape[-1] == 4):
    91	            raise ValueError(f"Boxes shape must be (N, 4) or (B, N, 4) when {mode} mode. Got {boxes.shape}.")
    92	    else:
    93	        raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
    94	
    95	    boxes = boxes if boxes.is_floating_point() else boxes.float()
    96	    boxes = boxes if batched else boxes.unsqueeze(0)
    97	
    98	    if mode.startswith("vertices"):
    99	        if mode == "vertices":
   100	            # Avoid passing reference
   101	            quadrilaterals = boxes.clone()
   102	        elif mode == "vertices_plus":
   103	            quadrilaterals = boxes.clone()  # TODO: perform +1
   104	        else:
   105	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   106	        validate_boxes or validate_bbox(quadrilaterals)
   107	    elif mode.startswith("xy"):
   108	        if mode == "xyxy":
   109	            height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1], boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0]
   110	        elif mode == "xyxy_plus":
   111	            height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1, boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
   112	        elif mode == "xywh":
   113	            height, width = boxes[..., 3], boxes[..., 2]
   114	        else:
   115	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   116	
   117	        if validate_boxes:
   118	            if (width <= 0).any():
   119	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative widths or 0.")
   120	            if (height <= 0).any():
   121	                raise ValueError("Some boxes have negative heights or 0.")
   122	
   123	        xmin, ymin = boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1]
   124	        quadrilaterals = _boxes_to_polygons(xmin, ymin, width, height)
   125	    else:
   126	        raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   127	
   128	    quadrilaterals = quadrilaterals if batched else quadrilaterals.squeeze(0)
   129	
   130	    return quadrilaterals

   287	    def to_tensor(
   288	        self, mode: Optional[str] = None, as_padded_sequence: bool = False
   289	    ) -> Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]]:
   290	        r"""Cast :class:`Boxes` to a tensor. ``mode`` controls which 2D boxes format should be use to represent boxes
   291	        in the tensor.
   292	
   293	        Args:
   294	            mode: the output box format. It could be:
   295	
   296	                * 'xyxy': boxes are defined as ``xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax`` where ``width = xmax - xmin`` and
   297	                  ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   298	                * 'xyxy_plus': similar to 'xyxy' mode but where box width and length are defined as
   299	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``.
   300	                * 'xywh': boxes are defined as ``xmin, ymin, width, height`` where ``width = xmax - xmin``
   301	                  and ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   302	                * 'vertices': boxes are defined by their vertices points in the following ``clockwise`` order:
   303	                  *top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left*. Vertices coordinates are in (x,y) order. Finally,
   304	                  box width and height are defined as ``width = xmax - xmin`` and ``height = ymax - ymin``.
   305	                * 'vertices_plus': similar to 'vertices' mode but where box width and length are defined as
   306	                  ``width = xmax - xmin + 1`` and ``height = ymax - ymin + 1``. ymin + 1``.
   307	            as_padded_sequence: whether to keep the pads for a list of boxes. This parameter is only valid
   308	                if the boxes are from a box list.
   309	
   310	        Returns:
   311	            Boxes tensor in the ``mode`` format. The shape depends with the ``mode`` value:
   312	
   313	                * 'vertices' or 'verticies_plus': :math:`(N, 4, 2)` or :math:`(B, N, 4, 2)`.
   314	                * Any other value: :math:`(N, 4)` or :math:`(B, N, 4)`.
   315	
   316	        Examples:
   317	            >>> boxes_xyxy = torch.as_tensor([[0, 3, 1, 4], [5, 1, 8, 4]])
   318	            >>> boxes = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes_xyxy)
   319	            >>> assert (boxes_xyxy == boxes.to_tensor(mode='xyxy')).all()
   320	        """
   321	        batched_boxes = self._data if self._is_batched else self._data.unsqueeze(0)
   322	
   323	        boxes: Union[torch.Tensor, List[torch.Tensor]]
   324	
   325	        # Create boxes in xyxy_plus format.
   326	        boxes = torch.stack([batched_boxes.amin(dim=-2), batched_boxes.amax(dim=-2)], dim=-2).view(
   327	            batched_boxes.shape[0], batched_boxes.shape[1], 4
   328	        )
   329	
   330	        if mode is None:
   331	            mode = self.mode
   332	
   333	        mode = mode.lower()
   334	
   335	        if mode in ("xyxy", "xyxy_plus"):
   336	            pass
   337	        elif mode in ("xywh", "vertices", "vertices_plus"):
   338	            height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1, boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
   339	            boxes[..., 2] = width
   340	            boxes[..., 3] = height
   341	        else:
   342	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown mode {mode}")
   343	
   344	        if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):
   345	            offset = torch.as_tensor([0, 0, 1, 1], device=boxes.device, dtype=boxes.dtype)
   346	            boxes = boxes + offset
   347	
   348	        if mode.startswith('vertices'):
   349	            boxes = _boxes_to_polygons(boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1], boxes[..., 2], boxes[..., 3])
   350	
   351	        if self._N is not None and not as_padded_sequence:
   352	            boxes = list(torch.nn.functional.pad(
   353	                o, (len(o.shape) - 1) * [0, 0] + [0, - n]) for o, n in zip(boxes, self._N))
   354	        else:
   355	            boxes = boxes if self._is_batched else boxes.squeeze(0)
   356	        return boxes

    60	def _boxes_to_polygons(
    61	    xmin: torch.Tensor, ymin: torch.Tensor, width: torch.Tensor, height: torch.Tensor
    62	) -> torch.Tensor:
    63	    if not xmin.ndim == ymin.ndim == width.ndim == height.ndim == 2:
    64	        raise ValueError("We expect to create a batch of 2D boxes (quadrilaterals) in vertices format (B, N, 4, 2)")
    65	
    66	    # Create (B,N,4,2) with all points in top left position of boxes
    67	    polygons = torch.zeros((xmin.shape[0], xmin.shape[1], 4, 2), device=xmin.device, dtype=xmin.dtype)
    68	    polygons[..., 0] = xmin.unsqueeze(-1)
    69	    polygons[..., 1] = ymin.unsqueeze(-1)
    70	    # Shift top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left points to the right coordinates
    71	    polygons[..., 1, 0] += width - 1  # Top right
    72	    polygons[..., 2, 0] += width - 1  # Bottom right
    73	    polygons[..., 2, 1] += height - 1  # Bottom right
    74	    polygons[..., 3, 1] += height - 1  # Bottom left
    75	    return polygons
    76	
</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I can see the issue clearly! Let me trace through what happens:

1. When we call `Boxes.from_tensor(boxes)` with default mode "xyxy", it goes through `_boxes_to_quadrilaterals` with mode="xyxy"
2. This calculates height and width as: `height = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1]` and `width = boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0]`
3. Then it calls `_boxes_to_polygons(xmin, ymin, width, height)` 
4. In `_boxes_to_polygons`, it creates polygons with:
   - Top-right x: `xmin + width - 1` (line 71)
   - Bottom-right x: `xmin + width - 1` (line 72)  
   - Bottom-right y: `ymin + height - 1` (line 73)
   - Bottom-left y: `ymin + height - 1` (line 74)

So for a box [355,10, 660,250]:
- xmin=355, ymin=10
- width = 660-355 = 305
- height = 250-10 = 240
- Top-right x = 355 + 305 - 1 = 659 (but should be 660!)
- Bottom-right x = 355 + 305 - 1 = 659 (but should be 660!)
- Bottom-right y = 10 + 240 - 1 = 249 (but should be 250!)
- Bottom-left y = 10 + 240 - 1 = 249 (but should be 250!)

This explains why when we convert back, we get [355,10, 661,251] instead of [355,10, 660,250].

The issue is in the `to_tensor` method. When converting from vertices back to xyxy format, the logic is wrong.

Looking at lines 335-346:
- Line 326-328: Creates boxes in xyxy_plus format by taking min/max of vertices
- Lines 335-342: Handles different modes
- Lines 344-346: For "xyxy" and "vertices" modes, it ADDS an offset of [0, 0, 1, 1]

But this is wrong! When we have vertices that were created from xyxy coordinates, we shouldn't be adding 1 to the max coordinates. 

Actually, let me retrace this more carefully...

When we call `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")`:
1. It goes to `_boxes_to_quadrilaterals` with mode="vertices" 
2. Since mode starts with "vertices", it just clones the vertices (line 101)
3. So the internal representation is the vertices themselves

Then when we call `to_tensor("vertices")`:
1. It gets the min/max of the vertices to create xyxy_plus format (lines 326-328)
2. For our example vertices [[[355,10], [660,10], [660,250], [355,250]]], the min is [355,10] and max is [660,250]
3. So boxes becomes [355,10,660,250] in xyxy_plus format
4. Since mode="vertices", it goes to lines 344-346 and ADDS [0,0,1,1], making it [355,10,661,251]
5. Then it calls `_boxes_to_polygons` with these coordinates

That's the bug! The offset addition in lines 344-346 is incorrect for the "vertices" mode when converting back from vertices.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 4. import torch
from kornia.geometry.boxes import Boxes

def test_vertices_roundtrip():
    """Test that converting boxes to vertices and back preserves the original values."""
    
    # Original boxes in xyxy format: [xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax]
    boxes = torch.tensor([[[355, 10, 660, 250], [10, 125, 200, 400]]])
    print("Original boxes (xyxy):")
    print(boxes)
    
    # Convert to Boxes object (default mode is xyxy)
    boxes_obj = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes)
    print("\nInternal representation (vertices from xyxy):")
    print(boxes_obj.data)
    
    # Convert to vertices format
    vertices = boxes_obj.to_tensor('vertices')
    print("\nVertices from original boxes:")
    print(vertices)
    
    # Convert vertices back to Boxes object with mode="vertices"
    boxes_from_vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")
    print("\nBoxes object from vertices:")
    print(boxes_from_vertices.data)
    
    # Convert back to vertices
    vertices_back = boxes_from_vertices.to_tensor("vertices")
    print("\nVertices from boxes (mode='vertices'):")
    print(vertices_back)
    
    print("\nAre they equal?", torch.equal(vertices, vertices_back))
    
    # Show the difference
    print("\nDifference:")
    diff = vertices_back - vertices
    print(diff)
    
    # Check if the issue exists
    if not torch.equal(vertices, vertices_back):
        print("BUG CONFIRMED: Round-trip conversion fails!")
        return False
    else:
        print("SUCCESS: Round-trip conversion works correctly!")
        return True

def test_simple_case():
    """Test with a simpler case to understand the issue better."""
    
    # Simple box: [0, 0, 1, 1] represents a 2x2 box from (0,0) to (1,1) inclusive
    boxes = torch.tensor([[[0, 0, 1, 1]]])
    print("\n=== Simple Case ===")
    print("Original boxes (xyxy):")
    print(boxes)
    
    # Convert to vertices
    vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes).to_tensor('vertices')
    print("\nVertices from original boxes:")
    print(vertices)
    
    # Convert back from vertices
    vertices_back = Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")
    print("\nVertices back from vertices mode:")
    print(vertices_back)
    
    print("\nEqual?", torch.equal(vertices, vertices_back))
    print("Expected vertices should be:")
    print("[[[[0., 0.], [1., 0.], [1., 1.], [0., 1.]]]]")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_simple_case()

import torch
from kornia.geometry.boxes import Boxes, _boxes_to_quadrilaterals, _boxes_to_polygons

def debug_conversion_process():
    """Debug the exact conversion process step by step."""
    
    # Original boxes in xyxy format: [xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax]  
    boxes_xyxy = torch.tensor([[[355, 10, 660, 250]]])
    print("=== Step 1: Original boxes (xyxy) ===")
    print(boxes_xyxy)
    print(f"Shape: {boxes_xyxy.shape}")
    
    # Step 2: Convert to quadrilaterals using _boxes_to_quadrilaterals
    print("\n=== Step 2: Call _boxes_to_quadrilaterals with mode='xyxy' ===")
    quadrilaterals = _boxes_to_quadrilaterals(boxes_xyxy, mode="xyxy")
    print("Result:")
    print(quadrilaterals)
    print(f"Shape: {quadrilaterals.shape}")
    
    # Let's manually calculate what should happen
    print("\n=== Manual calculation ===")
    xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax = boxes_xyxy[0, 0, 0], boxes_xyxy[0, 0, 1], boxes_xyxy[0, 0, 2], boxes_xyxy[0, 0, 3]
    print(f"xmin={xmin}, ymin={ymin}, xmax={xmax}, ymax={ymax}")
    width = xmax - xmin  # 660 - 355 = 305
    height = ymax - ymin  # 250 - 10 = 240
    print(f"width = xmax - xmin = {xmax} - {xmin} = {width}")
    print(f"height = ymax - ymin = {ymax} - {ymin} = {height}")
    
    print("\nCalling _boxes_to_polygons with these values:")
    polygons = _boxes_to_polygons(xmin.unsqueeze(0).unsqueeze(0), ymin.unsqueeze(0).unsqueeze(0), 
                                 width.unsqueeze(0).unsqueeze(0), height.unsqueeze(0).unsqueeze(0))
    print(polygons)
    
    # Step 3: Create Boxes object
    print("\n=== Step 3: Create Boxes object ===")
    boxes_obj = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes_xyxy)
    print("Internal data:")
    print(boxes_obj.data)
    
    # Step 4: Convert to vertices using to_tensor
    print("\n=== Step 4: Convert to vertices using to_tensor('vertices') ===")
    vertices = boxes_obj.to_tensor('vertices')
    print(vertices)
    
    # Step 5: Convert vertices back to Boxes with mode="vertices"
    print("\n=== Step 5: Convert vertices back with mode='vertices' ===")
    boxes_from_vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")
    print("Internal data:")
    print(boxes_from_vertices.data)

    print("\n=== Step 6: Convert back to vertices ===")
    vertices_back = boxes_from_vertices.to_tensor("vertices")
    print(vertices_back)
    
    print("\n=== Difference Analysis ===")
    diff = vertices_back - vertices

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly state the problem and solution:

### PROBLEM ANALYSIS

There are actually two related issues:

**Issue 1: Initial conversion from xyxy to vertices is inconsistent**
When we call `Boxes.from_tensor(xyxy_boxes)`, it internally converts to vertices using `_boxes_to_polygons` which subtracts 1 from width/height to create the polygon. But when we later call `to_tensor('vertices')`, it applies a correction to make the coordinates match the expected vertices.

**Issue 2: Round-trip conversion with mode="vertices" fails**
When we call `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")` followed by `to_tensor("vertices")`, it incorrectly adds an offset of [0, 0, 1, 1] in the `to_tensor` method.

### LOCATING THE BUG

The bug is in the `to_tensor` method, specifically lines 344-346:

```python
if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):
    offset = torch.as_tensor([0, 0, 1, 1], device=boxes.device, dtype=boxes.dtype)
    boxes = boxes + offset
```

This offset addition is incorrect for the "vertices" mode. When we have actual vertex data and want to convert it back to vertices, we shouldn't be adding 1 to the max coordinates.

### HOW THE TEST REPRODUCES THE ISSUE

1. We start with xyxy boxes: [355, 10, 660, 250]
2. Convert to Boxes object, which internally stores vertices: [[355,10], [659,10], [659,249], [355,249]]
3. Convert to vertices with `to_tensor('vertices')`: This corrects the vertices to [[355,10], [660,10], [660,250], [355,250]]
4. Convert back with `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")`: This creates a Boxes object with the correct vertices
5. Convert to vertices again with `to_tensor("vertices")`: This incorrectly adds [0,0,1,1] offset, resulting in [[355,10], [661,10], [661,251], [355,251]]

### BEST PRACTICES TO CONSIDER

1. The fix should maintain backward compatibility
2. Should not break existing functionality for other modes
3. Should be minimal and focused
4. Should preserve the intended semantics of different modes

### HOW TO FIX THE PROBLEM

The issue is in the offset logic in `to_tensor`. Looking at the logic:

1. Lines 326-328: Create boxes in xyxy_plus format by taking min/max of the internal vertex representation
2. Lines 335-342: Handle different modes by adjusting the box coordinates
3. Lines 344-346: Apply offset for certain modes

For "vertices" mode, when we already have vertex data, we shouldn't apply the xyxy offset. The offset is meant for converting from internal xyxy_plus representation to other formats, but when the mode is "vertices" and we're returning vertices, we should skip this offset.

Actually, let me re-read the logic more carefully...

Looking at the flow:
1. Internal representation is always vertices
2. `to_tensor` converts from internal vertices to various output formats
3. First, it computes the bounding box in xyxy_plus format (min/max of vertices)
4. Then it adjusts based on the target mode
5. For "vertices" and "xyxy", it adds [0,0,1,1] offset
6. Then for "vertices" mode, it calls `_boxes_to_polygons` to create vertices

The problem is that the offset addition in step 5 is wrong for "vertices" mode. When we want to output vertices and we already computed the correct bounding box from the existing vertices, we shouldn't add any offset.

Wait, let me trace through this even more carefully by looking at what the internal representation actually contains.

From my debug output:
- Input xyxy: [355, 10, 660, 250]  
- Internal vertices after from_tensor: [[355,10], [659,10], [659,249], [355,249]]
- When we call to_tensor('vertices'), min=[355,10], max=[659,249]
- So boxes = [355,10,659,249] in xyxy_plus format
- Then offset [0,0,1,1] is added, making it [355,10,660,250]  
- Then _boxes_to_polygons is called with these coordinates

This actually seems correct for the first conversion!

The real issue is when we start with actual vertices:
- Input vertices: [[355,10], [660,10], [660,250], [355,250]]
- Internal representation: same vertices
- When calling to_tensor('vertices'): min=[355,10], max=[660,250]  
- So boxes = [355,10,660,250] in xyxy_plus format
- Then offset [0,0,1,1] is added, making it [355,10,661,251]
- Then _boxes_to_polygons is called with these wrong coordinates

So the bug is that the offset should NOT be applied when the target mode is "vertices" AND we're coming from actual vertex data.

But wait, how does the system know if it's coming from vertex data vs computed from xyxy?

Looking at the constructor, the mode is stored. When we call `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")`, the mode gets set to "vertices".

But in `to_tensor`, the logic doesn't consider the source mode, only the target mode.

Actually, let me check what happens in step 330-331:

```python
if mode is None:
    mode = self.mode
```

So when we call `to_tensor("vertices")`, the target mode is "vertices", but `self.mode` (the source mode) could be different.

The key insight is: when the TARGET mode is "vertices", we shouldn't apply the xyxy offset regardless of the source mode, because we're going to reconstruct the vertices anyway using `_boxes_to_polygons`.

No wait, that's not right either. Let me think about what the offset is supposed to do.

Looking at the documentation:
- xyxy: xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax where width = xmax - xmin and height = ymax - ymin
- xyxy_plus: similar but width = xmax - xmin + 1 and height = ymax - ymin + 1
- vertices: defined by vertices with width = xmax - xmin and height = ymax - ymin

So the offset is meant to convert between these representations.

But there's confusion here. The internal computation in lines 326-328 computes the ACTUAL bounding box of the vertices:
- min coordinate = actual minimum vertex coordinate  
- max coordinate = actual maximum vertex coordinate

This gives us the true bounding box. Then depending on the target mode, we may need to adjust it.

For xyxy target: we want width = xmax - xmin, height = ymax - ymin. But our current boxes has width = xmax - xmin + 1, height = ymax - ymin + 1 (because it came from amax - amin + 1 adjustment earlier). So we need to subtract 1, which is done by adding offset [0,0,-1,-1]. Wait no, the offset is [0,0,1,1] which adds 1.

This is confusing. Let me trace through what the actual values are.

Actually, let me re-read lines 337-341:

```python
elif mode in ("xywh", "vertices", "vertices_plus"):
    height, width = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1, boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1
    boxes[..., 2] = width  
    boxes[..., 3] = height
```

And lines 344-346:
```python
if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):

    boxes = boxes + offset
```

import torch

def trace_to_tensor_logic():
    """Trace the exact logic in to_tensor method."""
    
    # Simulate what happens in to_tensor method
    # Let's say we have vertices: [[355,10], [660,10], [660,250], [355,250]]
    vertices = torch.tensor([[[[355., 10.], [660., 10.], [660., 250.], [355., 250.]]]])

    # Step 1: batched_boxes (line 321)
    batched_boxes = vertices  # Already batched

    # This takes min and max of vertices along the vertex dimension (-2)
    min_coords = batched_boxes.amin(dim=-2)  # min over the 4 vertices 
    max_coords = batched_boxes.amax(dim=-2)  # max over the 4 vertices
    print(f"\nStep 2 - min_coords: {min_coords}")
    print(f"Step 2 - max_coords: {max_coords}")
    
    # Stack them and reshape
    boxes_stacked = torch.stack([min_coords, max_coords], dim=-2)
    boxes = boxes_stacked.view(batched_boxes.shape[0], batched_boxes.shape[1], 4)
    print(f"Step 2 - boxes (xyxy_plus format): {boxes}")

    mode = "vertices"  # Target mode
    
    print(f"\nStep 3 - Processing mode: {mode}")
    
    # Lines 335-342: Mode-specific adjustments
    if mode in ("xyxy", "xyxy_plus"):
        print("Taking xyxy/xyxy_plus branch - no adjustment")
        pass
    elif mode in ("xywh", "vertices", "vertices_plus"):
        print("Taking xywh/vertices/vertices_plus branch")
        height = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1  # ymax - ymin + 1
        width = boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1   # xmax - xmin + 1
        print(f"  Computed height: {height[0,0]} = {boxes[0,0,3]} - {boxes[0,0,1]} + 1")
        print(f"  Computed width: {width[0,0]} = {boxes[0,0,2]} - {boxes[0,0,0]} + 1")
        boxes[..., 2] = width
        boxes[..., 3] = height
        print(f"  Updated boxes: {boxes}")
        print(f"  Now represents: [xmin, ymin, width, height] = [{boxes[0,0,0]}, {boxes[0,0,1]}, {boxes[0,0,2]}, {boxes[0,0,3]}]")
    
    # Lines 344-346: Offset adjustment  
    if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):
        print(f"\nStep 4 - Applying offset for mode {mode}")
        offset = torch.as_tensor([0, 0, 1, 1], dtype=boxes.dtype)
        print(f"  Offset: {offset}")
        boxes_before = boxes.clone()
        boxes = boxes + offset
        print(f"  Boxes before offset: {boxes_before}")
        print(f"  Boxes after offset: {boxes}")
        print(f"  This makes it: [xmin, ymin, xmax+1, ymax+1]")
    
    # Lines 348-349: Convert to polygons if vertices mode
    if mode.startswith('vertices'):
        print(f"\nStep 5 - Converting to polygons for mode {mode}")
        xmin, ymin = boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1]
        width, height = boxes[..., 2], boxes[..., 3]
        print(f"  xmin={xmin[0,0]}, ymin={ymin[0,0]}, width={width[0,0]}, height={height[0,0]}")
        
        # This is _boxes_to_polygons logic
        polygons = torch.zeros((xmin.shape[0], xmin.shape[1], 4, 2), dtype=xmin.dtype)
        polygons[..., 0] = xmin.unsqueeze(-1)  # top-left x
        polygons[..., 1] = ymin.unsqueeze(-1)  # top-left y
        polygons[..., 1, 0] += width - 1  # top-right x = xmin + width - 1
        polygons[..., 2, 0] += width - 1  # bottom-right x = xmin + width - 1  
        polygons[..., 2, 1] += height - 1 # bottom-right y = ymin + height - 1
        polygons[..., 3, 1] += height - 1 # bottom-left y = ymin + height - 1
        
        print(f"  Resulting polygons: {polygons}")
        print(f"  Top-right x = {xmin[0,0]} + {width[0,0]} - 1 = {xmin[0,0] + width[0,0] - 1}")

        print(f"  Bottom-left y = {ymin[0,0]} + {height[0,0]} - 1 = {ymin[0,0] + height[0,0] - 1}")

**THE BUG**: In step 4, when the mode is "vertices", it applies an offset of [0, 0, 1, 1] to the boxes. But this is wrong because:

1. After step 3, we have [xmin, ymin, width, height] = [355, 10, 306, 241]
2. The offset adds [0, 0, 1, 1], making it [355, 10, 307, 242]  
3. Then `_boxes_to_polygons` uses width=307 and height=242 to create the vertices
4. This results in coordinates like 355 + 307 - 1 = 661 instead of the correct 660

**THE FIX**: The offset should NOT be applied when mode="vertices". Looking at the logic, the offset is meant for converting from one box format to another, but when we're going to vertices mode, we should use the width and height as-is.

Looking at the modes:
- For "xyxy": we want [xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax] format where width = xmax - xmin
- For "xywh": we want [xmin, ymin, width, height] format where width and height are already computed  
- For "vertices": we want to create vertices from [xmin, ymin, width, height]

The offset [0, 0, 1, 1] should only be applied for "xyxy" mode, not "vertices" mode.

Looking at line 344: `if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):` - this is wrong!

import torch
from kornia.geometry.boxes import Boxes

def check_semantics():
    """Check what the semantic meaning should be."""
    
    # Original boxes: [355, 10, 660, 250]
    # This should represent a box from (355,10) to (660,250) inclusive
    # So the width should be 660 - 355 + 1 = 306 pixels wide
    # And height should be 250 - 10 + 1 = 241 pixels tall
    
    boxes_xyxy = torch.tensor([[[355, 10, 660, 250]]])
    print("Original boxes (xyxy):", boxes_xyxy)
    print("Expected interpretation: box from (355,10) to (660,250) inclusive")
    print("Expected width:", 660 - 355 + 1, "pixels")
    print("Expected height:", 250 - 10 + 1, "pixels")
    
    # Convert to vertices
    vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes_xyxy).to_tensor('vertices')
    print("\nActual vertices:", vertices)
    
    # Check vertex positions
    top_left = vertices[0,0,0]      # [355, 10]
    top_right = vertices[0,0,1]     # [659, 10] 
    bottom_right = vertices[0,0,2]  # [659, 249]
    bottom_left = vertices[0,0,3]   # [355, 249]
    
    print(f"\nVertex positions:")
    print(f"  Top-left: {top_left}")
    print(f"  Top-right: {top_right}")  
    print(f"  Bottom-right: {bottom_right}")
    print(f"  Bottom-left: {bottom_left}")
    
    # Calculate actual width and height from vertices
    actual_width = top_right[0] - top_left[0] + 1  # 659 - 355 + 1 = 305
    actual_height = bottom_left[1] - top_left[1] + 1  # 249 - 10 + 1 = 240
    
    print(f"\nActual dimensions from vertices:")
    print(f"  Width: {actual_width} pixels (expected 306)")
    print(f"  Height: {actual_height} pixels (expected 241)")
    
    # This suggests there's still an issue in the forward conversion
    # The vertices should be [355,10], [660,10], [660,250], [355,250]

def test_with_vertices_mode():
    """Test providing the expected vertices directly."""
    
    # Provide the vertices that we think are correct
    expected_vertices = torch.tensor([[[[355., 10.], [660., 10.], [660., 250.], [355., 250.]]]])
    print("Expected vertices:", expected_vertices)
    
    # Convert using mode="vertices"
    boxes_obj = Boxes.from_tensor(expected_vertices, mode="vertices")
    print("Boxes object internal data:", boxes_obj.data)
    
    # Convert back to vertices
    vertices_back = boxes_obj.to_tensor("vertices")
    print("Vertices back:", vertices_back)
    
    print("Equal?", torch.equal(expected_vertices, vertices_back))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    check_semantics()
    print("\n" + "="*50)

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>This reveals that there are actually TWO issues:

1. **Main Issue (FIXED)**: The round-trip conversion with mode="vertices" now works correctly ✅

2. **Secondary Issue**: The forward conversion from xyxy to vertices produces vertices that are off by 1. The vertices should be [660,10] and [660,250] but they come out as [659,10] and [659,249].

However, looking at the issue description again, the main complaint was about the round-trip failing. Let me re-read it carefully...

The issue says that when you do:
1. `Boxes.from_tensor(boxes)` - creates vertices 
2. `.to_tensor('vertices')` - gets vertices
3. `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")` - creates boxes from vertices  
4. `.to_tensor("vertices")` - gets vertices again

Steps 2 and 4 should give the same result, but they didn't. Now they do, so the main issue is fixed.

The secondary issue about the forward conversion might be a separate design decision about coordinate conventions. Let me check if this is documented or tested anywhere.

    # Which represents vertices: top-left=(1,2), top-right=(2,2), bottom-right=(2,3), bottom-left=(1,3)
    # Wait, that doesn't seem right. Let me check the ordering.
    
    # Looking at the vertices: [A, B, C, D] = [[2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3], [1, 2]]
    # According to the docs: clockwise order: top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left
    # So: A=top-left=(2,2), B=top-right=(2,3), C=bottom-right=(1,3), D=bottom-left=(1,2)
    # But this doesn't make sense - top-right should have higher x than top-left

    print("\nBoxes object created")
    print("Internal data:", box.data)

import torch
from kornia.geometry.boxes import Boxes

def analyze_original_issue():
    """Analyze the exact sequence from the issue report."""
    
    print("=== ORIGINAL ISSUE ANALYSIS ===")
    
    # Step 1: Original boxes in xyxy format
    boxes = torch.tensor([[[355,10, 660,250], [10,125, 200,400]]], dtype=torch.float32) 
    print("1. Original boxes (xyxy format):")
    print(boxes)
    
    # Step 2: Convert to Boxes object (this internally creates vertices)
    boxes_obj1 = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes)
    print("\n2. Boxes object from xyxy (internal vertices representation):")
    print(boxes_obj1.data)
    
    # Step 3: Convert to vertices format  
    vertices = boxes_obj1.to_tensor('vertices')
    print("\n3. Vertices from original boxes:")
    print(vertices)
    
    # Step 4: Convert vertices back to Boxes with mode="vertices"
    boxes_obj2 = Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")
    print("\n4. Boxes object from vertices (mode='vertices'):")
    print(boxes_obj2.data)
    print(f"   Mode: {boxes_obj2.mode}")
    
    # Step 5: Convert back to vertices
    vertices_back = boxes_obj2.to_tensor("vertices")
    print("\n5. Vertices from boxes (mode='vertices'):")
    print(vertices_back)
    
    print("\n6. Comparison:")
    print("   Equal?", torch.equal(vertices, vertices_back))
    if not torch.equal(vertices, vertices_back):
        print("   DIFFERENCE DETECTED!")
        print("   Difference:", vertices_back - vertices)
    
    print("\n=== WHAT SHOULD HAPPEN ===")
    print("The issue is that step 5 produces different vertices than step 3.")
    print("This happens because of the offset logic in to_tensor method.")

def trace_to_tensor_for_vertices_mode():
    """Trace what happens in to_tensor when mode='vertices' and source has mode='vertices'."""
    
    print("\n=== TRACING to_tensor with mode='vertices' ===")
    
    # Start with the vertices from step 3 above
    vertices = torch.tensor([[[[355.,  10.], [660.,  10.], [660., 250.], [355., 250.]],
                              [[ 10., 125.], [200., 125.], [200., 400.], [ 10., 400.]]]])
    print("Input vertices:")
    print(vertices)
    
    # Create Boxes object with these vertices (mode will be vertices_plus by default)
    boxes_obj = Boxes(vertices)
    print(f"Created Boxes object with mode: {boxes_obj.mode}")
    print("Internal data:")
    print(boxes_obj.data)
    
    # Now trace the to_tensor('vertices') call
    print("\n--- Tracing to_tensor('vertices') ---")
    
    # Get internal data
    batched_boxes = boxes_obj.data if boxes_obj._is_batched else boxes_obj.data.unsqueeze(0)
    print(f"batched_boxes shape: {batched_boxes.shape}")
    
    # Compute bounding box (lines 326-328)
    min_coords = batched_boxes.amin(dim=-2)
    max_coords = batched_boxes.amax(dim=-2) 
    boxes_stacked = torch.stack([min_coords, max_coords], dim=-2)
    boxes = boxes_stacked.view(batched_boxes.shape[0], batched_boxes.shape[1], 4)
    print(f"Computed bounding box (xyxy_plus format): {boxes}")
    print(f"  Box 1: [{boxes[0,0,0]}, {boxes[0,0,1]}, {boxes[0,0,2]}, {boxes[0,0,3]}]")
    print(f"  Box 2: [{boxes[0,1,0]}, {boxes[0,1,1]}, {boxes[0,1,2]}, {boxes[0,1,3]}]")
    
    # Process mode="vertices" (lines 337-341)
    mode = "vertices"
    print(f"\nProcessing mode: {mode}")
    if mode in ("xywh", "vertices", "vertices_plus"):
        height = boxes[..., 3] - boxes[..., 1] + 1  # ymax - ymin + 1
        width = boxes[..., 2] - boxes[..., 0] + 1   # xmax - xmin + 1
        print(f"  Computed height: {height}")
        print(f"  Computed width: {width}")
        boxes[..., 2] = width
        boxes[..., 3] = height
        print(f"  Updated boxes to [xmin, ymin, width, height]: {boxes}")
    
    # Apply offset (lines 344-346) - THIS IS THE PROBLEMATIC PART
    if mode in ("xyxy", "vertices"):
        print(f"\nApplying offset for mode {mode}")
        offset = torch.as_tensor([0, 0, 1, 1], dtype=boxes.dtype)
        print(f"  Offset: {offset}")
        boxes_before = boxes.clone()
        boxes = boxes + offset
        print(f"  Boxes before: {boxes_before}")
        print(f"  Boxes after: {boxes}")
        print(f"  This changes [xmin, ymin, width, height] to [xmin, ymin, width+1, height+1]")
    
    # Convert to polygons (lines 348-349)
    if mode.startswith('vertices'):
        print(f"\nConverting to polygons for mode {mode}")
        xmin, ymin = boxes[..., 0], boxes[..., 1]
        width, height = boxes[..., 2], boxes[..., 3]
        print(f"  xmin={xmin}, ymin={ymin}, width={width}, height={height}")
        
        # This recreates the vertices
        from kornia.geometry.boxes import _boxes_to_polygons
        result_polygons = _boxes_to_polygons(xmin, ymin, width, height)
        print(f"  Result polygons: {result_polygons}")
