[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Good, so the mode is correctly set to "vertices". Now I understand the issue completely.

The problem is that the offset logic is applied based on the TARGET mode, not considering whether the source data is already in vertex format.

Let me think about the proper fix. The key insight is:

When we call `to_tensor("vertices")`, we need to consider:
1. What is the CURRENT internal representation? (vertices, xyxy, etc.)  
2. What is the TARGET output format? (vertices, xyxy, etc.)

The current logic applies offset based only on the target mode. But for vertices→vertices conversion, no offset should be applied.

However, the issue is more subtle. Looking at the logic flow:

1. `to_tensor` always computes the bounding box of the current vertices (min/max coordinates)
2. Then it adjusts based on target mode
3. For vertices target mode, it adds [0,0,1,1] offset  
4. Then it calls `_boxes_to_polygons` to create new vertices

The issue is step 3. When the target is vertices, we shouldn't add that offset.

self = <test.geometry.test_boxes.TestBoxes2D object at 0x74f57c0c3d00>, shape = (1, 4), device = device(type='cpu'), dtype = torch.float32

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('shape', [(1, 4), (1, 1, 4)])
    def test_boxes_to_tensor(self, shape: Tuple[int], device, dtype):
        # quadrilateral with randomized vertices to reflect possible transforms.
        box = Boxes(torch.as_tensor([[[2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3], [1, 2]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape, 2))

        expected_box_xyxy = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 3, 4]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_box_xyxy_plus = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 2, 3]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_box_xywh = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 2, 2]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_vertices = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [3, 2], [3, 4], [1, 4]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(
            *shape, 2
        )
        expected_vertices_plus = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(
            *shape, 2
        )

        boxes_xyxy = box.to_tensor(mode='xyxy')
        boxes_xyxy_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='xyxy_plus')
        boxes_xywh = box.to_tensor(mode='xywh')
        boxes_vertices = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices')
        boxes_vertices_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices_plus')

        assert boxes_xyxy.shape == expected_box_xyxy.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xyxy, expected_box_xyxy)

        assert boxes_xyxy_plus.shape == expected_box_xyxy_plus.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xyxy_plus, expected_box_xyxy_plus)

        assert boxes_xywh.shape == expected_box_xywh.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xywh, expected_box_xywh)

        assert boxes_vertices.shape == expected_vertices.shape  # type: ignore
>       assert_allclose(boxes_vertices, expected_vertices)

Let me understand what the offset is doing:
1. It's applied to boxes that are in [xmin, ymin, width, height] format
2. It adds [0, 0, 1, 1] making it [xmin, ymin, width+1, height+1] 
3. Then _boxes_to_polygons is called

But wait, that doesn't make sense. If we're going to vertices mode, we should be passing [xmin, ymin, width, height] directly to _boxes_to_polygons.

Let me re-read the logic more carefully.

Actually, let me step back and think about what the expected behavior should be conceptually:

1. Internal representation is always vertices
2. to_tensor converts from internal vertices to various output formats
3. First step: compute bounding box of internal vertices (min/max) - this gives [xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax] 
4. This is implicitly in "xyxy_plus" format because it comes from actual vertex coordinates
5. Then convert to target format

So when target mode is "vertices", we should:
1. Take the actual bounding box [xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax] 
2. Convert to [xmin, ymin, width, height] where width = xmax - xmin + 1, height = ymax - ymin + 1
3. Pass this to _boxes_to_polygons

But the issue is that the current logic adds an extra offset AFTER step 2.

Let me trace through the working test case to see what SHOULD happen:

Input vertices: [[2,2], [2,3], [1,3], [1,2]]
Min: [1,2], Max: [2,3]
So bounding box is [1,2,2,3] in xyxy_plus format

For vertices mode:
- Convert to [xmin, ymin, width, height] = [1, 2, 2-1+1, 3-2+1] = [1, 2, 2, 2]
- But the expected output is [[1,2], [3,2], [3,4], [1,4]]
- This corresponds to bounding box [1,2,3,4] 
- Which means [xmin, ymin, width, height] = [1, 2, 3, 2] for width and [1, 2, 2, 3] for height
- Wait, that's confusing.

Let me look at the expected vertices: [[1,2], [3,2], [3,4], [1,4]]
This is: top-left=(1,2), top-right=(3,2), bottom-right=(3,4), bottom-left=(1,4)
So bounding box is from (1,2) to (3,4)
Width = 3-1+1 = 3, Height = 4-2+1 = 3

So expected [xmin, ymin, width, height] = [1, 2, 3, 3]

But computed bounding box from input vertices is [1,2,2,3] (min to max)
Computed width = 2-1+1 = 2, height = 3-2+1 = 2
So [xmin, ymin, width, height] = [1, 2, 2, 2]

There's a discrepancy of 1 in both width and height.

This suggests that for the vertices mode, there's an expectation that the bounding box should be expanded by 1 in each direction.

But that doesn't make sense for the round-trip issue I'm trying to fix.

Let me think of a compromise approach:

Maybe the offset should be applied conditionally - only when the source mode is NOT "vertices" or "vertices_plus"?

Actually, let me look at this from a different angle. The real issue described in the bug report is about round-trip consistency. Let me make sure that's working first, and then see if I can make both work.

The key insight is: when converting vertices->vertices, no adjustment should be made.
When converting other formats->vertices, the existing logic should apply.

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('shape', [(1, 4), (1, 1, 4)])
    def test_boxes_to_tensor(self, shape: Tuple[int], device, dtype):
        # quadrilateral with randomized vertices to reflect possible transforms.
        box = Boxes(torch.as_tensor([[[2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3], [1, 2]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape, 2))

        expected_box_xyxy = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 3, 4]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_box_xyxy_plus = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 2, 3]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_box_xywh = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 2, 2]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_vertices = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [3, 2], [3, 4], [1, 4]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(
            *shape, 2
        )
        expected_vertices_plus = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(
            *shape, 2
        )

        boxes_xyxy = box.to_tensor(mode='xyxy')
        boxes_xyxy_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='xyxy_plus')
        boxes_xywh = box.to_tensor(mode='xywh')
        boxes_vertices = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices')
        boxes_vertices_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices_plus')

        assert boxes_xyxy.shape == expected_box_xyxy.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xyxy, expected_box_xyxy)

        assert boxes_xyxy_plus.shape == expected_box_xyxy_plus.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xyxy_plus, expected_box_xyxy_plus)

        assert boxes_xywh.shape == expected_box_xywh.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xywh, expected_box_xywh)

Let me trace through what happens mathematically:

1. We start with vertices `[[355,10], [660,10], [660,250], [355,250]]`
2. When we call `Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices")`, it stores these vertices directly
3. When we call `to_tensor("vertices")`:
   - Computes bounding box: min=[355,10], max=[660,250] → [355,10,660,250]
   - This is in xyxy_plus format (because it's actual min/max of vertices)
   - Converts to [xmin,ymin,width,height]: width=660-355+1=306, height=250-10+1=241 → [355,10,306,241]
   - Applies offset [0,0,1,1]: [355,10,307,242]  
   - Calls _boxes_to_polygons(355,10,307,242) which creates vertices with:
     - top-right x = 355 + 307 - 1 = 661
     - bottom-right y = 10 + 242 - 1 = 251

But the issue is that we started with the CORRECT vertices, so we shouldn't be recomputing them with adjusted dimensions.

The key insight is: when the source is already vertices and the target is vertices, we should return the vertices unchanged.

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('shape', [(1, 4), (1, 1, 4)])
    def test_boxes_to_tensor(self, shape: Tuple[int], device, dtype):
        # quadrilateral with randomized vertices to reflect possible transforms.
        box = Boxes(torch.as_tensor([[[2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3], [1, 2]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape, 2))

        expected_box_xyxy = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 3, 4]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_box_xyxy_plus = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 2, 3]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_box_xywh = torch.as_tensor([[1, 2, 2, 2]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(*shape)
        expected_vertices = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [3, 2], [3, 4], [1, 4]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(
            *shape, 2
        )
        expected_vertices_plus = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3]]], device=device, dtype=dtype).view(
            *shape, 2
        )

        boxes_xyxy = box.to_tensor(mode='xyxy')
        boxes_xyxy_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='xyxy_plus')
        boxes_xywh = box.to_tensor(mode='xywh')
        boxes_vertices = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices')
        boxes_vertices_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices_plus')

        assert boxes_xyxy.shape == expected_box_xyxy.shape  # type: ignore
        assert_allclose(boxes_xyxy, expected_box_xyxy)

           :func:`torch.testing.assert_allclose` is deprecated since ``1.12`` and will be removed in a future release.

def debug_issue_scenario():
    print("=== ISSUE SCENARIO ===")
    boxes = torch.tensor([[[355,10, 660,250], [10,125, 200,400]]], dtype=torch.float32)

    vertices = boxes_obj1.to_tensor('vertices')

    print(vertices)

## Phase 7. VERIFICATION: Comprehensive Testing<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/kornia__kornia__0.6/comprehensive_test.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

import torch
from kornia.geometry.boxes import Boxes

def test_comprehensive_round_trip():
    """Test various round-trip scenarios to ensure robustness."""
    
    print("=== COMPREHENSIVE ROUND-TRIP TESTS ===")
    
    # Test 1: Original issue case
    print("\n1. Original issue case:")
    boxes = torch.tensor([[[355,10, 660,250], [10,125, 200,400]]], dtype=torch.float32) 
    vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(boxes).to_tensor('vertices')
    vertices_back = Boxes.from_tensor(vertices, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")
    success1 = torch.equal(vertices, vertices_back)
    print(f"   Success: {success1}")
    
    # Test 2: Simple case
    print("\n2. Simple case:")
    simple_boxes = torch.tensor([[[0, 0, 1, 1]]], dtype=torch.float32)
    simple_vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(simple_boxes).to_tensor('vertices')
    simple_back = Boxes.from_tensor(simple_vertices, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")
    success2 = torch.equal(simple_vertices, simple_back)
    print(f"   Success: {success2}")
    
    # Test 3: Different modes round-trip
    print("\n3. Different modes round-trip:")
    test_boxes = torch.tensor([[[10, 20, 30, 40]]], dtype=torch.float32)
    
    # xyxy -> vertices -> vertices
    v1 = Boxes.from_tensor(test_boxes, mode="xyxy").to_tensor('vertices')
    v2 = Boxes.from_tensor(v1, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")
    success3 = torch.equal(v1, v2)
    print(f"   xyxy->vertices->vertices: {success3}")
    
    # xywh -> vertices -> vertices  
    v3 = Boxes.from_tensor(test_boxes, mode="xywh").to_tensor('vertices')
    v4 = Boxes.from_tensor(v3, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")
    success4 = torch.equal(v3, v4)
    print(f"   xywh->vertices->vertices: {success4}")
    
    # Test 4: Batch processing
    print("\n4. Batch processing:")
    batch_boxes = torch.tensor([
        [[10, 20, 30, 40], [50, 60, 70, 80]],
        [[5, 15, 25, 35], [40, 50, 60, 70]]
    ], dtype=torch.float32)
    batch_vertices = Boxes.from_tensor(batch_boxes).to_tensor('vertices')
    batch_back = Boxes.from_tensor(batch_vertices, mode="vertices").to_tensor("vertices")
    success5 = torch.equal(batch_vertices, batch_back)
    print(f"   Batch round-trip: {success5}")
    
    # Test 5: Ensure we didn't break other conversions
    print("\n5. Other conversions still work:")
    test_box = Boxes.from_tensor(test_boxes)
    
    # xyxy conversion
    xyxy_result = test_box.to_tensor('xyxy')
    expected_xyxy = torch.tensor([[10, 20, 31, 41]], dtype=torch.float32)  # +1 due to convention
    success6 = torch.allclose(xyxy_result, expected_xyxy)
    print(f"   xyxy conversion: {success6}")
    
    # xywh conversion  
    xywh_result = test_box.to_tensor('xywh')
    expected_xywh = torch.tensor([[10, 20, 21, 21]], dtype=torch.float32)  # width/height
    success7 = torch.allclose(xywh_result, expected_xywh)
    print(f"   xywh conversion: {success7}")
    
    all_success = all([success1, success2, success3, success4, success5, success6, success7])
    print(f"\n=== ALL TESTS PASSED: {all_success} ===")
    return all_success

def test_backward_compatibility():
    """Ensure existing behavior is preserved."""
    
    print("\n=== BACKWARD COMPATIBILITY TESTS ===")
    
    # Test the case from the existing test suite
    print("\n1. Existing test case behavior:")
    box_vertices = torch.as_tensor([[[2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3], [1, 2]]], dtype=torch.float32)
    box = Boxes(box_vertices)
    result = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices')
    expected = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [3, 2], [3, 4], [1, 4]]], dtype=torch.float32)
    success1 = torch.equal(result, expected)
    print(f"   Vertices expansion: {success1}")
    
    # Test vertices_plus mode
    print("\n2. Vertices plus mode:")
    result_plus = box.to_tensor(mode='vertices_plus')
    expected_plus = torch.as_tensor([[[1, 2], [2, 2], [2, 3], [1, 3]]], dtype=torch.float32)
    success2 = torch.equal(result_plus, expected_plus)
    print(f"   Vertices plus: {success2}")
    
    all_success = success1 and success2
    print(f"\n=== COMPATIBILITY MAINTAINED: {all_success} ===")
    return all_success

if __name__ == "__main__":

+        # Special handling for vertices mode to avoid double-adjustment in round-trip conversion
+        if mode == "vertices" and self.mode == "vertices":
+            # For vertices-to-vertices conversion where source is exactly 'vertices',
+            # return the data directly to maintain round-trip consistency
+            boxes = self._data if self._is_batched else self._data.unsqueeze(0)
+            if self._N is not None and not as_padded_sequence:
+                boxes = list(torch.nn.functional.pad(
+                    o, (len(o.shape) - 1) * [0, 0] + [0, - n]) for o, n in zip(boxes, self._N))
+            else:
+                boxes = boxes if self._is_batched else boxes.squeeze(0)
+            return boxes
